People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Smaller, harder, no matter what I try.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the right way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these feelings of sadness and heaviness most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I do still show up and do what I’m supposed to do. But most days there’s this dull heaviness with me.\n\nEven if I’m at the community center or out in my garden—places that used to make me feel lighter—I’m sort of just… going through the motions. Smiling and acting pleasant, but it feels pasted on. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of interest in activities you once enjoyed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it a significant loss, exactly. I mean, I still do things when I can, and I don’t want to sound like I’ve just given up on everything.\n\nBut… some things don’t pull me in the way they used to. I notice I’m less eager, maybe, and it takes more effort to get myself to go. Is that what you mean by loss of interest?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and heaviness are mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d say… moderate, I think. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m probably making more of it than I should, but… I’ve been having a harder time keeping my mind on things lately. Even when I’m at the community center or out in my garden—places that usually make me feel better—I’m still sort of just going through the motions.\n\nI can smile and keep up with people, but it feels a bit pasted on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years now, I think. It started after a close friend of mine died. I kept telling myself it was just normal grief and that I should be handling it better, but it hasn’t really lifted the way I thought it would.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure I’ve paid close enough attention, honestly. Maybe some nights are better than others, but that’s probably pretty normal, isn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and heaviness are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, more than I like to admit. I’m still getting through my days, but at the credit office I’ve started putting off some client follow-ups, which isn’t really like me. And socially, I’ve kind of stepped back from gallery events and the walking-club outings I used to make a point of going to.\n\nIt’s not that I’ve stopped everything, but… it does get in the way, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: I try not to think of it as hopeless, exactly… I keep telling myself things could still turn around, and maybe they will. But underneath that, there’s this tired feeling like… maybe this is just how my life is going to be now. Smaller, harder, no matter what I try.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the right way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these feelings of sadness and heaviness most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I do still show up and do what I’m supposed to do. But most days there’s this dull heaviness with me.\n\nEven if I’m at the community center or out in my garden—places that used to make me feel lighter—I’m sort of just… going through the motions. Smiling and acting pleasant, but it feels pasted on. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of interest in activities you once enjoyed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it a significant loss, exactly. I mean, I still do things when I can, and I don’t want to sound like I’ve just given up on everything.\n\nBut… some things don’t pull me in the way they used to. I notice I’m less eager, maybe, and it takes more effort to get myself to go. Is that what you mean by loss of interest?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and heaviness are mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d say… moderate, I think. I don’t want to call it severe, because I am still getting through my days and doing what I need to do.\n\nBut it’s more than mild. Most days there’s that dull heaviness sitting with me, and even in places that used to lift me up, like the community center or my garden, I’m kind of just going through the motions with a smile on. Does moderate sound right for that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. There are things I still do, and I wouldn’t want to say I get no enjoyment from them. Like I can have a nice moment here or there.\n\nBut “look forward to” feels harder to answer. I think I mostly keep up with things because they’re part of my routine, or because I know I should. Is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that bring you a sense of pleasure or joy?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe small things, sometimes. Like I can appreciate a pretty day, or a pleasant conversation, and I don’t want to make it sound like nothing ever feels good.\n\nBut joy, exactly… that’s harder for me to point to. I guess I notice little moments more than whole activities that really bring me pleasure. Is that an okay way to answer it?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you no longer take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. The community center used to lift me up more, and being in my garden did too. I still go, and I still do the things, so it’s not like I’ve stopped completely.\n\nBut most days there’s this dull heaviness, and even in those places I feel like I’m kind of going through the motions with a smile on. I don’t know if that counts as not taking pleasure exactly, but it doesn’t feel the way it used to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m probably making more of it than I should, but… I’ve been having a harder time keeping my mind on things lately. At work, I’ll be looking at a loan worksheet or a client follow-up list, and I catch myself rereading the same line over and over.\n\nThen I put off making calls because even simple decisions feel harder than they ought to. I just don’t feel as sharp as I used to, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days there’s kind of a dull heaviness there. Even when I’m at the community center or out in my garden—places that usually make me feel better—I’m still sort of just going through the motions.\n\nI can smile and keep up with people, but it feels a bit pasted on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years now, I think. It started after a close friend of mine died. I kept telling myself it was just normal grief and that I should be handling it better, but it hasn’t really lifted the way I thought it would.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure I’ve paid close enough attention, honestly. I don’t think there’s been anything very dramatic with my appetite or sleep that I could point to. Maybe some nights are better than others, but that’s probably pretty normal, isn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and heaviness are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, more than I like to admit. I’m still getting through my days, but at the credit office I’ve started putting off some client follow-ups, which isn’t really like me. And socially, I’ve kind of stepped back from gallery events and the walking-club outings I used to make a point of going to.\n\nIt’s not that I’ve stopped everything, but… it does get in the way, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: I try not to think of it as hopeless, exactly… I keep telling myself things could still turn around, and maybe they will. But underneath that, there’s this tired feeling like… maybe this is just how my life is going to be now. Smaller, harder, no matter what I try.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the right way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these feelings of sadness and heaviness most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I do still show up and do what I’m supposed to do. But most days there’s this dull heaviness with me.\n\nEven if I’m at the community center or out in my garden—places that used to make me feel lighter—I’m sort of just… going through the motions. Smiling and acting pleasant, but it feels pasted on. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of interest in activities you once enjoyed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it a significant loss, exactly. I mean, I still do things when I can, and I don’t want to sound like I’ve just given up on everything.\n\nBut… some things don’t pull me in the way they used to. I notice I’m less eager, maybe, and it takes more effort to get myself to go. Is that what you mean by loss of interest?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and heaviness are mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d say… moderate, I think. I don’t want to call it severe, because I am still getting through my days and doing what I need to do.\n\nBut it’s more than mild. Most days there’s that dull heaviness sitting with me, and even in places that used to lift me up, like the community center or my garden, I’m kind of just going through the motions with a smile on. Does moderate sound right for that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. There are things I still do, and I wouldn’t want to say I get no enjoyment from them. Like I can have a nice moment here or there.\n\nBut “look forward to” feels harder to answer. I think I mostly keep up with things because they’re part of my routine, or because I know I should. Is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that bring you a sense of pleasure or joy?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe small things, sometimes. Like I can appreciate a pretty day, or a pleasant conversation, and I don’t want to make it sound like nothing ever feels good.\n\nBut joy, exactly… that’s harder for me to point to. I guess I notice little moments more than whole activities that really bring me pleasure. Is that an okay way to answer it?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you no longer take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. The community center used to lift me up more, and being in my garden did too. I still go, and I still do the things, so it’s not like I’ve stopped completely.\n\nBut most days there’s this dull heaviness, and even in those places I feel like I’m kind of going through the motions with a smile on. I don’t know if that counts as not taking pleasure exactly, but it doesn’t feel the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find give you a sense of pleasure?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I don’t want to say there’s no pleasure at all. Sometimes at the community center, or when I’m out in the garden, there can be a small nice moment.\n\nBut most days there’s still this dull heaviness with me, even there. Those places used to lift me up more. Now I feel like I’m mostly going through the motions, smiling because I’m supposed to. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Not completely, I don’t think… I mean, I can’t honestly say I enjoy them the way I used to.

I don’t want to call it severe, because I am still getting through my days and doing what I need to do.\n\nBut it’s more than mild. Most days there’s that dull heaviness sitting with me, and even in places that used to lift me up, like the community center or my garden, I’m kind of just going through the motions with a smile on. Does moderate sound right for that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. There are things I still do, and I wouldn’t want to say I get no enjoyment from them. Like I can have a nice moment here or there.\n\nBut “look forward to” feels harder to answer. I think I mostly keep up with things because they’re part of my routine, or because I know I should. Is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that bring you a sense of pleasure or joy?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe small things, sometimes. Like I can appreciate a pretty day, or a pleasant conversation, and I don’t want to make it sound like nothing ever feels good.\n\nBut joy, exactly… that’s harder for me to point to. I guess I notice little moments more than whole activities that really bring me pleasure. Is that an okay way to answer it?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you no longer take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. The community center used to lift me up more, and being in my garden did too. I still go, and I still do the things, so it’s not like I’ve stopped completely.\n\nBut most days there’s this dull heaviness, and even in those places I feel like I’m kind of going through the motions with a smile on. I don’t know if that counts as not taking pleasure exactly, but it doesn’t feel the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find give you a sense of pleasure?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I don’t want to say there’s no pleasure at all. Sometimes at the community center, or when I’m out in the garden, there can be a small nice moment.\n\nBut most days there’s still this dull heaviness with me, even there. Those places used to lift me up more. Now I feel like I’m mostly going through the motions, smiling because I’m supposed to. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and now find you no longer enjoy at all?\n\nPatient: Not completely, I don’t think… I mean, I can’t honestly say I enjoy them the way I used to.\n\nThe community center and my garden used to really lift me up. Now, most days, there’s this dull heaviness sort of sitting with me even when I’m there. I still show up, and I try to smile and be pleasant, but it feels more like I’m going through the motions.\n\nSo maybe not “no longer enjoy at all,” but it’s… much less than it was. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Even when I’m at the community center or out in my garden—places that usually make me feel better—I’m still sort of just going through the motions.\n\nI can smile and keep up with people, but it feels a bit pasted on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years now, I think. It started after a close friend of mine died. I kept telling myself it was just normal grief and that I should be handling it better, but it hasn’t really lifted the way I thought it would.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure I’ve paid close enough attention, honestly. No… I don’t think anything feels as strong as it used to, if I’m being honest.

I still go to the community center, and I still spend time in my garden, and there are little moments that are nice. But most days there’s this dull heaviness there with me, even in those places. I feel like I’m sort of going through the motions, smiling because I should.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.1, F32.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m probably making more of it than I should, but… I’ve been having a harder time keeping my mind on things lately. At work, I’ll be looking at a loan worksheet or a client follow-up list, and I catch myself rereading the same line over and over.\n\nThen I put off making calls because even simple decisions feel harder than they ought to. I just don’t feel as sharp as I used to, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days there’s kind of a dull heaviness there. Even when I’m at the community center or out in my garden—places that usually make me feel better—I’m still sort of just going through the motions.\n\nI can smile and keep up with people, but it feels a bit pasted on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years now, I think. It started after a close friend of mine died. I kept telling myself it was just normal grief and that I should be handling it better, but it hasn’t really lifted the way I thought it would.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure I’ve paid close enough attention, honestly. I don’t think there’s been anything very dramatic with my appetite or sleep that I could point to. Maybe some nights are better than others, but that’s probably pretty normal, isn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and heaviness are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, more than I like to admit. I’m still getting through my days, but at the credit office I’ve started putting off some client follow-ups, which isn’t really like me. And socially, I’ve kind of stepped back from gallery events and the walking-club outings I used to make a point of going to.\n\nIt’s not that I’ve stopped everything, but… it does get in the way, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: I try not to think of it as hopeless, exactly… I keep telling myself things could still turn around, and maybe they will. But underneath that, there’s this tired feeling like… maybe this is just how my life is going to be now. Smaller, harder, no matter what I try.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the right way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these feelings of sadness and heaviness most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I do still show up and do what I’m supposed to do. But most days there’s this dull heaviness with me.\n\nEven if I’m at the community center or out in my garden—places that used to make me feel lighter—I’m sort of just… going through the motions. Smiling and acting pleasant, but it feels pasted on. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of interest in activities you once enjoyed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it a significant loss, exactly. I mean, I still do things when I can, and I don’t want to sound like I’ve just given up on everything.\n\nBut… some things don’t pull me in the way they used to. I notice I’m less eager, maybe, and it takes more effort to get myself to go. Is that what you mean by loss of interest?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and heaviness are mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d say… moderate, I think. I don’t want to call it severe, because I am still getting through my days and doing what I need to do.\n\nBut it’s more than mild. Most days there’s that dull heaviness sitting with me, and even in places that used to lift me up, like the community center or my garden, I’m kind of just going through the motions with a smile on. Does moderate sound right for that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. There are things I still do, and I wouldn’t want to say I get no enjoyment from them. Like I can have a nice moment here or there.\n\nBut “look forward to” feels harder to answer. I think I mostly keep up with things because they’re part of my routine, or because I know I should. Is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that bring you a sense of pleasure or joy?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe small things, sometimes. Like I can appreciate a pretty day, or a pleasant conversation, and I don’t want to make it sound like nothing ever feels good.\n\nBut joy, exactly… that’s harder for me to point to. I guess I notice little moments more than whole activities that really bring me pleasure. Is that an okay way to answer it?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you no longer take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. The community center used to lift me up more, and being in my garden did too. I don’t know if that counts as not taking pleasure exactly, but it doesn’t feel the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find give you a sense of pleasure?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I don’t want to say there’s no pleasure at all. It’s… it’s been there since my close friend died, a little over two years ago now.

At first I thought it was just grief, and maybe I kept telling myself it would ease up soon. And it does have stronger and lighter days, I guess, but that dull heaviness has kind of stayed with me in some form since then.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.1, F32.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m probably making more of it than I should, but… I’ve been having a harder time keeping my mind on things lately. At work, I’ll be looking at a loan worksheet or a client follow-up list, and I catch myself rereading the same line over and over.\n\nThen I put off making calls because even simple decisions feel harder than they ought to. I just don’t feel as sharp as I used to, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days there’s kind of a dull heaviness there. Even when I’m at the community center or out in my garden—places that usually make me feel better—I’m still sort of just going through the motions.\n\nI can smile and keep up with people, but it feels a bit pasted on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years now, I think. It started after a close friend of mine died. I kept telling myself it was just normal grief and that I should be handling it better, but it hasn’t really lifted the way I thought it would.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure I’ve paid close enough attention, honestly. I don’t think there’s been anything very dramatic with my appetite or sleep that I could point to. Maybe some nights are better than others, but that’s probably pretty normal, isn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and heaviness are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, more than I like to admit. I’m still getting through my days, but at the credit office I’ve started putting off some client follow-ups, which isn’t really like me. And socially, I’ve kind of stepped back from gallery events and the walking-club outings I used to make a point of going to.\n\nIt’s not that I’ve stopped everything, but… it does get in the way, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: I try not to think of it as hopeless, exactly… I keep telling myself things could still turn around, and maybe they will. But underneath that, there’s this tired feeling like… maybe this is just how my life is going to be now. Smaller, harder, no matter what I try.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the right way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these feelings of sadness and heaviness most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I do still show up and do what I’m supposed to do. But most days there’s this dull heaviness with me.\n\nEven if I’m at the community center or out in my garden—places that used to make me feel lighter—I’m sort of just… going through the motions. Smiling and acting pleasant, but it feels pasted on. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of interest in activities you once enjoyed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it a significant loss, exactly. I mean, I still do things when I can, and I don’t want to sound like I’ve just given up on everything.\n\nBut… some things don’t pull me in the way they used to. I notice I’m less eager, maybe, and it takes more effort to get myself to go. Is that what you mean by loss of interest?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and heaviness are mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d say… moderate, I think. I don’t want to call it severe, because I am still getting through my days and doing what I need to do.\n\nBut it’s more than mild. Most days there’s that dull heaviness sitting with me, and even in places that used to lift me up, like the community center or my garden, I’m kind of just going through the motions with a smile on. Does moderate sound right for that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. There are things I still do, and I wouldn’t want to say I get no enjoyment from them. Like I can have a nice moment here or there.\n\nBut “look forward to” feels harder to answer. I think I mostly keep up with things because they’re part of my routine, or because I know I should. Is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that bring you a sense of pleasure or joy?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe small things, sometimes. Like I can appreciate a pretty day, or a pleasant conversation, and I don’t want to make it sound like nothing ever feels good.\n\nBut joy, exactly… that’s harder for me to point to. I guess I notice little moments more than whole activities that really bring me pleasure. Is that an okay way to answer it?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you no longer take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. The community center used to lift me up more, and being in my garden did too. I still go, and I still do the things, so it’s not like I’ve stopped completely.\n\nBut most days there’s this dull heaviness, and even in those places I feel like I’m kind of going through the motions with a smile on. I don’t know if that counts as not taking pleasure exactly, but it doesn’t feel the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find give you a sense of pleasure?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I don’t want to say there’s no pleasure at all. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.1, F32.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m probably making more of it than I should, but… I’ve been having a harder time keeping my mind on things lately. At work, I’ll be looking at a loan worksheet or a client follow-up list, and I catch myself rereading the same line over and over.\n\nThen I put off making calls because even simple decisions feel harder than they ought to. I just don’t feel as sharp as I used to, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days there’s kind of a dull heaviness there. Even when I’m at the community center or out in my garden—places that usually make me feel better—I’m still sort of just going through the motions.\n\nI can smile and keep up with people, but it feels a bit pasted on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years now, I think. It started after a close friend of mine died. I kept telling myself it was just normal grief and that I should be handling it better, but it hasn’t really lifted the way I thought it would.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure I’ve paid close enough attention, honestly. I don’t think there’s been anything very dramatic with my appetite or sleep that I could point to. Maybe some nights are better than others, but that’s probably pretty normal, isn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and heaviness are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, more than I like to admit. I’m still getting through my days, but at the credit office I’ve started putting off some client follow-ups, which isn’t really like me. And socially, I’ve kind of stepped back from gallery events and the walking-club outings I used to make a point of going to.\n\nIt’s not that I’ve stopped everything, but… it does get in the way, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: I try not to think of it as hopeless, exactly… I keep telling myself things could still turn around, and maybe they will. But underneath that, there’s this tired feeling like… maybe this is just how my life is going to be now. Smaller, harder, no matter what I try.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the right way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these feelings of sadness and heaviness most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I do still show up and do what I’m supposed to do. But most days there’s this dull heaviness with me.\n\nEven if I’m at the community center or out in my garden—places that used to make me feel lighter—I’m sort of just… going through the motions. Smiling and acting pleasant, but it feels pasted on. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of interest in activities you once enjoyed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it a significant loss, exactly. I mean, I still do things when I can, and I don’t want to sound like I’ve just given up on everything.\n\nBut… some things don’t pull me in the way they used to. I notice I’m less eager, maybe, and it takes more effort to get myself to go. Is that what you mean by loss of interest?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and heaviness are mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d say… moderate, I think. I don’t want to call it severe, because I am still getting through my days and doing what I need to do.\n\nBut it’s more than mild. Most days there’s that dull heaviness sitting with me, and even in places that used to lift me up, like the community center or my garden, I’m kind of just going through the motions with a smile on. Does moderate sound right for that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. There are things I still do, and I wouldn’t want to say I get no enjoyment from them. Like I can have a nice moment here or there.\n\nBut “look forward to” feels harder to answer. I think I mostly keep up with things because they’re part of my routine, or because I know I should. Is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that bring you a sense of pleasure or joy?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe small things, sometimes. Like I can appreciate a pretty day, or a pleasant conversation, and I don’t want to make it sound like nothing ever feels good.\n\nBut joy, exactly… that’s harder for me to point to. I guess I notice little moments more than whole activities that really bring me pleasure. Is that an okay way to answer it?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you no longer take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. The community center used to lift me up more, and being in my garden did too. I still go, and I still do the things, so it’s not like I’ve stopped completely.\n\nBut most days there’s this dull heaviness, and even in those places I feel like I’m kind of going through the motions with a smile on. I don’t know if that counts as not taking pleasure exactly, but it doesn’t feel the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find give you a sense of pleasure?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I don’t want to say there’s no pleasure at all. Sometimes at the community center, or when I’m out in the garden, there can be a small nice moment.\n\nBut most days there’s still this dull heaviness with me, even there. Those places used to lift me up more. Now I feel like I’m mostly going through the motions, smiling because I’m supposed to. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and now find you no longer enjoy at all?\n\nPatient: Not completely, I don’t think… I mean, I can’t honestly say I enjoy them the way I used to.\n\nThe community center and my garden used to really lift me up. Now, most days, there’s this dull heaviness sort of sitting with me even when I’m there. I still show up, and I try to smile and be pleasant, but it feels more like I’m going through the motions.\n\nSo maybe not “no longer enjoy at all,” but it’s… much less than it was. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and find pleasure in, even if it's just a little?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little. Sometimes at the community center, or when I’m out in the garden, there can be a small nice moment.\n\nBut most days there’s still this dull heaviness with me, even there. Those places used to lift me up more. Now I feel like I’m mostly going through the motions, smiling because I’m supposed to. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and now find you no longer enjoy at all?\n\nPatient: Not completely, I don’t think… I mean, I can’t honestly say I enjoy them the way I used to.\n\nThe community center and my garden used to really lift me up. Now, most days, there’s this dull heaviness sort of sitting with me even when I’m there. I still show up, and I try to smile and be pleasant, but it feels more like I’m going through the motions.\n\nSo maybe not “no longer enjoy at all,” but it’s… much less than it was. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and find pleasure in, even if it's just a little?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little. I still get something from the community center sometimes, and from being in my garden. I don’t want to make it sound like there’s no good feeling at all.\n\nIt’s just that most days there’s this dull heaviness sitting with me, even in those places. They used to lift me up more, and now I catch myself kind of going through the motions with a smile on. But yes, there are still small moments, I suppose. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find give you a sense of pleasure or enjoyment that's as strong as it was before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think anything feels as strong as it used to, if I’m being honest.\n\nI still go to the community center, and I still spend time in my garden, and there are little moments that are nice. But most days there’s this dull heaviness there with me, even in those places. I feel like I’m sort of going through the motions, smiling because I should.\n\nI’m sorry, I don’t mean to sound overly negative. It’s just… it doesn’t lift me the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about when you first started feeling this dull heaviness, was it constantly present since your friend passed away or did it come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s been there since my close friend died, a little over two years ago now.\n\nAt first I thought it was just grief, and maybe I kept telling myself it would ease up soon. And it does have stronger and lighter days, I guess, but that dull heaviness has kind of stayed with me in some form since then.\n\nI don’t know if I’m explaining that right. It hasn’t been dramatic every single minute, but it hasn’t really gone away either.\n\nDoctor: Has this dull heaviness you've been feeling been present for most of the past two years since your friend died, or has it come and gone?\n\nPatient: It’s been there for most of that time, yes… since my close friend died a little over two years ago.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like every single day has been exactly the same, because it hasn’t. Some days are lighter. But overall, it hasn’t really gone away. Does that answer what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Most of the time I can manage it, I think… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I still show up, I still go to the community center, I still spend time in my garden.

Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days there’s kind of a dull heaviness there. Even when I’m at the community center or out in my garden—places that usually make me feel better—I’m still sort of just going through the motions.\n\nI can smile and keep up with people, but it feels a bit pasted on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years now, I think. It started after a close friend of mine died. I kept telling myself it was just normal grief and that I should be handling it better, but it hasn’t really lifted the way I thought it would.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure I’ve paid close enough attention, honestly. I don’t think there’s been anything very dramatic with my appetite or sleep that I could point to. Maybe some nights are better than others, but that’s probably pretty normal, isn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and heaviness are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, more than I like to admit. I’m still getting through my days, but at the credit office I’ve started putting off some client follow-ups, which isn’t really like me. And socially, I’ve kind of stepped back from gallery events and the walking-club outings I used to make a point of going to.\n\nIt’s not that I’ve stopped everything, but… it does get in the way, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: I try not to think of it as hopeless, exactly… I keep telling myself things could still turn around, and maybe they will. But underneath that, there’s this tired feeling like… maybe this is just how my life is going to be now. Smaller, harder, no matter what I try.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the right way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these feelings of sadness and heaviness most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I do still show up and do what I’m supposed to do. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of interest in activities you once enjoyed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it a significant loss, exactly. I mean, I still do things when I can, and I don’t want to sound like I’ve just given up on everything.\n\nBut… some things don’t pull me in the way they used to. I notice I’m less eager, maybe, and it takes more effort to get myself to go. Is that what you mean by loss of interest?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and heaviness are mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d say… moderate, I think. I don’t want to call it severe, because I am still getting through my days and doing what I need to do.\n\nBut it’s more than mild. Most days there’s that dull heaviness sitting with me, and even in places that used to lift me up, like the community center or my garden, I’m kind of just going through the motions with a smile on. Does moderate sound right for that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. There are things I still do, and I wouldn’t want to say I get no enjoyment from them. Like I can have a nice moment here or there.\n\nBut “look forward to” feels harder to answer. I think I mostly keep up with things because they’re part of my routine, or because I know I should. Is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that bring you a sense of pleasure or joy?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe small things, sometimes. Like I can appreciate a pretty day, or a pleasant conversation, and I don’t want to make it sound like nothing ever feels good.\n\nBut joy, exactly… that’s harder for me to point to. I guess I notice little moments more than whole activities that really bring me pleasure. Is that an okay way to answer it?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you no longer take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. The community center used to lift me up more, and being in my garden did too. I still go, and I still do the things, so it’s not like I’ve stopped completely.\n\nBut most days there’s this dull heaviness, and even in those places I feel like I’m kind of going through the motions with a smile on. I don’t know if that counts as not taking pleasure exactly, but it doesn’t feel the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find give you a sense of pleasure?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I don’t want to say there’s no pleasure at all. Sometimes at the community center, or when I’m out in the garden, there can be a small nice moment.\n\nBut most days there’s still this dull heaviness with me, even there. Those places used to lift me up more. Now I feel like I’m mostly going through the motions, smiling because I’m supposed to. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and now find you no longer enjoy at all?\n\nPatient: Not completely, I don’t think… I mean, I can’t honestly say I enjoy them the way I used to.\n\nThe community center and my garden used to really lift me up. Now, most days, there’s this dull heaviness sort of sitting with me even when I’m there. I still show up, and I try to smile and be pleasant, but it feels more like I’m going through the motions.\n\nSo maybe not “no longer enjoy at all,” but it’s… much less than it was. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and find pleasure in, even if it's just a little?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little. I still get something from the community center sometimes, and from being in my garden. I don’t want to make it sound like there’s no good feeling at all.\n\nIt’s just that most days there’s this dull heaviness sitting with me, even in those places. They used to lift me up more, and now I catch myself kind of going through the motions with a smile on. But yes, there are still small moments, I suppose. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find give you a sense of pleasure or enjoyment that's as strong as it was before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think anything feels as strong as it used to, if I’m being honest.\n\nI still go to the community center, and I still spend time in my garden, and there are little moments that are nice. But most days there’s this dull heaviness there with me, even in those places. I feel like I’m sort of going through the motions, smiling because I should.\n\nI’m sorry, I don’t mean to sound overly negative. It’s just… it doesn’t lift me the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about when you first started feeling this dull heaviness, was it constantly present since your friend passed away or did it come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s been there since my close friend died, a little over two years ago now.\n\nAt first I thought it was just grief, and maybe I kept telling myself it would ease up soon. And it does have stronger and lighter days, I guess, but that dull heaviness has kind of stayed with me in some form since then.\n\nI don’t know if I’m explaining that right. It hasn’t been dramatic every single minute, but it hasn’t really gone away either.\n\nDoctor: Has this dull heaviness you've been feeling been present for most of the past two years since your friend died, or has it come and gone?\n\nPatient: It’s been there for most of that time, yes… since my close friend died a little over two years ago.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like every single day has been exactly the same, because it hasn’t. Some days are lighter. But overall, it hasn’t really gone away. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this dull heaviness you've been feeling is something that you can still manage to cope with most of the time, or is it often overwhelming?\n\nPatient: Most of the time I can manage it, I think… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I still show up, I still go to the community center, I still spend time in my garden.\n\nBut it’s there most days, that dull heaviness sitting with me. Even in those places that used to really lift me up, I feel like I’m kind of just going through the motions, with a smile pasted on. So… not always overwhelming, exactly, but it does wear me down. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
